===== \Assets\XML\AI_Variables_GlobalDefines.xml =====
----- Comment #2 -----
<!-- Recommended: 100 (BtS behavior)
			 If this percentage is less than 100, all AI personality values
			 from Civ4LeaderHeadInfos.xml are moved toward their median. Formula:
			 ((UWAI_PERSONALITY_PERCENT * x +
			 (100 - UWAI_PERSONALITY_PERCENT) * m) / 100
			 where x is a personality value of a given leader and m the median
			 over all leader personalities defined in XML (except the Barbarian
			 leader). E.g. UWAI_PERSONALITY_PERCENT=50 moves the values halfway
			 to the  median, making the AI personalities half as pronounced.
			 0 eliminates the personalities (apart from traits). Favorite civic
			 and religion are normally unaffected, but, in the special case of
			 UWAI_PERSONALITY_PERCENT=0, all leaders are set to having no
			 favorites. (They'll still "care for their brothers and sisters of
			 the faith" though.) UWAI_PERSONALITY_PERCENT>100 makes the
			 personalities more pronounced (not tested). Negative values invert
			 the personalities (not tested and not recommended). -->

===== \Assets\XML\Buildings\CIV4PlotLSystem.xml =====
----- Comment #15 -----
<!-- Attribute Overview

     On specific art:	ONE artref is picked as long as it satisfies all the subconstraints below
        Bonus
        Imporvement
        Scalars:
			bApplyRotation	- if set, the art will be rotated according to its cumulative rotation when placed
			bZMode_Ground	- if set, the builder will try to force the art to not z-write nor z-test
			bIsPartOfImprovement, bIsPartOfBonus - when provided once during a plot build on any placed artref, this will
			                                       prevent the LSystem from falling back to a fixed placement alg

	On nodes:
		RegionTest			- if value is 1, then the node will be tested against cut regions when expanded
							- if the value is 0 then this won't happen
							- if value is 0NW then the node won't be region tested or water tested
							- if the value is 1NW then the node will be region tested but won't be water tested

     On productions:
        Bonus			- can be multiple, if any met constraint met, then the production is expandable
        Improvement		- as with bonus
		Scalars:	
			bGoalOnly:1	- if true, then the production can only be expanded if we're in GOAL seek mode
						- note, this does not prevent child nodes from being visited by DFS or BFS
			bExtraGoalOnly:1	- same behavior as bGoalOnly, but when placing nodes defined by szExtra attributes on a goal artref

		-->

===== \Assets\XML\GameInfo\CIV4HandicapInfo.xml =====
----- Comment #20 -----
<!-- custom: As for iAIPerEraModifier translated to english with chrome from https://gforestshade.github.io/kujira/post/civ4handicapinfo/#iaipereramodifier:

<iAIPerEraModifier>
Game-wide settings
When the overall game difficulty is this difficulty,
the AI players will receive a (multiplicative) n% modifier to the following costs for each era they advance:

For example, when the Emperor AI (setting value: -5) is in the Renaissance era (the fourth era),
all the costs above will be modified by -20%.
The costs of world wonders, world projects, and world units are also affected by this attribute.

Example: (various costs are reduced by 5% each time an era advances)
<iAIPerEraModifier>-5</iAIPerEraModifier>

However, it doesnt seem to exist in base advciv xml and thus not in our advciv-sas, and maybe, is/seems to maybe have been replaced by this in base advciv now: iAIHandicapIncrementTurns

A global search about this tag/field shows this result in (adjust to your mod path C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Sid Meier's Civilization IV Beyond the Sword\Beyond the Sword\Mods\AdvCiv-SAS\CvGameCoreDLL\CvGame.cpp) :

// advc.251:
int CvGame::AIHandicapAdjustment() const
{
	int iIncrementTurns = GC.getInfo(getHandicapType()).getAIHandicapIncrementTurns();
	if (iIncrementTurns == 0)
		return 0;
	/*	Flip sign b/c we're dealing with cost modifiers that are supposed to decrease.
		Only if a negative AIHandicapIncrement is set in XML, the modifiers are
		supposed to increase. */
	return (-getGameTurn() * 100) / (iIncrementTurns * getSpeedPercent());
}

Based on this, i assume or and try to or and understandthat the higher this new (which i am still thankful for hehe) iAIHandicapIncrementTurns value is, the more the divider is higher, and thus the absolute value returned lower ;
as for the value itself the cost reduction will be seemingly higher the more the game turn is high from what i understand of it, while a higher iAIHandicapIncrementTurns will cause cost reduction to be lower.

So in other words, this adjusts for highest difficulties where AIs have massive boosts and tones these down a bit to a softer formula or something or maybe rather a smoother slower game pace from AIs at higher(st) difficulties, at least i am guessing it does this, i could or not be mistaken, what we'll do for advciv-sas is try to make the game more gradual, but also AIs stronger without (it) being a grind, so we'll none ideally or close to none cost reduction, except from natural (if i may say) or something adjustments as part of cities growing or such, but not sure we'll/'d handled it in such a way or how we'd do (i have an idea but will see how to adjust or reconsider as in recontemplate how i'd want AIs at higher difficulties relate to human players in terms of challenge (i want it higher) and no grind (i want less grind, competent AI, but ideally and if not possible too, forgot my sentence, in short,or not short indeed..., make difficulties more gradual and AI competitive where it does not have a huge unfair advantage ideally hard or painful to overcome but is consistently stronger more efficient or feels like so without being too ahead in tech or such like wonders or such, will see how handled and if possible ideally or not or yes or etc but that is/would be the goal indeed but not sure or yes or not but in all cases

So for now not changing it, may be adjusted in the future ideally indeed or may not be will see or may, but in all cases

update: it seems to be called in functions like CvPlayer::getProductionNeeded and CvPlayer::trainingModifierFromHandicap and CvTeam::getResearchCost and CvPlayer::getGrowthThreshold

like this for example for the first function i quoted

		int iAIModifier = //h.getAIPerEraModifier() * getCurrentEra()
				kGame.AIHandicapAdjustment();
		if(GC.getInfo(eProject).isWorldProject())
			iAIModifier += h.getAIWorldCreatePercent();
		else iAIModifier += h.getAICreatePercent();
		iProductionNeeded *= iAIModifier;

so this would explain why our AIs run away midgame with huge military despite the conservative and gradual handicap settings i tried to implement.

Since this interferes with how i want to balance the game, directly via the handicap modifiers like iAITrainPercent or iAIConstructPercent or such and have a balanced and fair game where AI plays well at all difficulties and gradually harder not exponentially and having a close hard fought game not this shit xd if i'm not mistaken, disabling it if i understood it correctly.

I asked chatgpt 5 to be sure and to confirm it as some of it was unclear to me, and indeed i mean thanks to it i see very clearly i should set this to 0 at all difficulties as i want it if i want reliable handicap settings. (Then adjust as i want fields like iAITrainPercent or such), but check if accurate.

Here is its explanation based on code samples i fed it too (and most of the above explanation as well):

beginning of quote
"
- Yes—you’ve read it right. iAIHandicapIncrementTurns adds a linear, time-based discount for AIs that stacks every so many turns (scaled by game speed).
- To disable it without recompiling, set iAIHandicapIncrementTurns to 0 in CIV4HandicapInfo.xml. Setting it to 100 does not disable it; it just makes the drip very slow.
"
end of quote

it also said:
beginning of quote
"
This is a (usually negative) percentage points value that gets added to the various AI Percent fields when costs are computed (train/construct/world variants, research, growth threshold, etc.). With a positive iAIHandicapIncrementTurns, the return value is negative and grows in magnitude over time ⇒ AI costs go down the longer the game runs.

Rule of thumb on Normal speed (getSpeedPercent()==100):
iAIHandicapIncrementTurns = 100 → about –1% per 100 turns

50 → –2% per 100 turns
33 → ≈ –3% per 100 turns
25 → –4% per 100 turns
20 → –5% per 100 turns

So across 300–400 turns, those add up (e.g., 20 yields roughly –15% to –20% total extra discount by late game). That’s likely a contributor to the midgame AI runaway you’re seeing even with conservative base handicap percents.
"
end of quote

And i didn't want to recompile so i asked it as such too, and it confirmed i mean that i can do 0 rather and no recompile
-->

===== \Assets\XML\Text\AdvCiv-SAS_Core.xml =====
----- Comment #30 -----
<!-- custom: 
Before starting, a note about translations:

If we define only the <English>My translation example</Example> tag without putting the other language tags, then (according to ChatGPT), the other languages will see the English version, no need to copy paste the same version many times, thanks ChatGPT
etc too maybe anyways.
Detailed explanations of ChatGPT below (i modified the formatting of it a bit but same content (i may have added Upper cases instead or Change a bit some sentences to connect them, should be mostly the same)).

1) For new content we add (for example if we were to add a new sniper unit (great memory ChatGPT thanks a lot )):
:
Quote:
"
What happens if you only define an XML entry in English?

If you only define one language (like English) in your XML (e.g., CIV4GameText.xml), and leave out translations for other languages, then:
Yes — other languages will see the English version.

Why?
Because Civ4's text system falls back to English if:
- A translation is missing for the current language.
- The player's selected language is not found in the XML at all.

So for example:
<TEXT>
	<Tag>TXT_KEY_UNIT_SNIPER</Tag>
	<English>Sniper</English>
</TEXT>

If the player has their game language set to French, Spanish, or anything else — they'll still see "Sniper" in English, because:
- The game didn’t find <French>, <German>, etc., for that tag.
- So it shows <English> as a fallback.

Exceptions / Notes:
- If the <English> field is missing entirely, the game will show the tag name (like TXT_KEY_UNIT_SNIPER) instead.
- If you include a <French> tag but leave it empty, it may show nothing or just whitespace — so it's better to leave other languages out entirely unless you have a proper translation.
"


2) For content already existing in the game (for example FEATURE_ICE):
Quoting ChatGPT again, thanks ChatGPT and etc thanks all too or not too etc, anyways:

Quote:
"
This gets into how overrides work in Civ4's XML system. Here's the breakdown:
You override an existing in-game text key (e.g. TXT_KEY_FEATURE_ICE) in your mod's XML:

<TEXT>
	<Tag>TXT_KEY_FEATURE_ICE</Tag>
	<English>Frozen Hell</English>
</TEXT>

But you do not include <French>, <German>, etc.

What happens in-game?
Civ4 will completely override the original text entry, even in non-English languages.

So:
- English players will see: "Frozen Hell"
- French players will not see the original Firaxis French translation — they’ll see "Frozen Hell" too.
- Other languages will also see your new English text, not the base game's translation.
"

Important Note:
Once you override a <Tag> in your mod, the original translations are no longer used, even if you only replace <English>.
That means:
- You're responsible for translations if you want to preserve them.
- If not, it's totally fine — Civ4 will fall back to your <English> string in all languages.

Pro Tip:
If you're just changing English and want to keep the original translations, you can rename the tag, like:

<Tag>TXT_KEY_FEATURE_ICE_ALT</Tag>

Then use TXT_KEY_FEATURE_ICE_ALT wherever you want (e.g. in your modified CIV4FeatureInfos.xml). That way:
- The original TXT_KEY_FEATURE_ICE stays untouched.
- You can localize or not as you like.

Summary:
What You Do										What Players See
Only override <English> of a base tag			All languages will see your English string
Don't touch a tag								Players see base game translations
Rename the tag									Original untouched; you're free to localize your version
"
-->

----- Comment #84 -----
<!-- custom: note about collateral damage calculation in advciv and by extension in our mod xd since i as of now didn't change it, according to chatgpt 5 thanks, and about whether it is additive or multiplicative or other, after feeding it our relevant code (which i found in CvUnit::collateralCombat if i was not mistaken in how i found it  (check if accurate as i don't know too much about these but i could or not be right), manual, and it doing a quite extensive web search:

"
Great catch digging into your own DLL + notes — that settles it.
In your AdvCiv/K-Mod base the effect is “mostly multiplicative”, not simple add-on:
	- The “collateral strength” is

	iCollateralStrength = baseCombatStr * collateralDamage() / 100

	If collateralDamage() == 0, that term is 0.

	- However, the function doesn’t bail if your unit has promotion-based bonus: it returns only if iCollateralStrength <= 0 && getExtraCollateralDamage() <= 0.
	- The actual damage per target is then computed from a ratio that still includes a non-zero iStrengthFactor based on the defender’s strength, and finally scaled by 100 + getExtraCollateralDamage() (your Collateral I, II, …).
	→ With base collateral 0, promotions still work, but the numbers are modest (exactly what your doc meant by “with the multiplicative formula, 0 base ⇒ Barrage does little”).

	So the mental model is:
	- Not 0 + 15 = 15%.
	- Also not 0 × 1.15 = 0% (i.e., “no effect”).
	- It’s “small baseline from the strength-ratio” × (1 + promo bonus), and capped by your MaxUnits and Limit.
"
-->

===== \Assets/XML/BasicInfos/BasicInfos notes (concept categories) =====
----- Comment #93 -----
My goals doing this are:
- rename "Concepts" to "Mods Info"
- rename "Game Concepts" to "Concepts (Outdated)"
- move all entries that are in "Mods Info (Outdated)"
(except BtS rules changes) to "Concepts (Outdated)"

Purpose:
- gather all Civ4 old data together (vanilla + BTS)
- use a new category for all mods rules changes (not balance changes
except if already written such as in bts rules changes)

I had figured out most of it, but could not make it work, so i asked
@f1rpo which gave me a very detailed answer that helped me solve this.

Credit goes to @f1rpo, current maintainer of AdvCiv, to have explained
to me (see below for the explanation quoted, that i added here with
@f1rpo's accord) in thorough detail how to achieve that, which helped
a lot. I had given to @f1rpo slightly different names for the categories
than the one i provided here at the "rename" bullet points above, but
the explanation should be mostly (almost entirely in fact) the same
(minus these name differences).

Beginning of quote:
"
Apparently,
TXT_KEY_PEDIA_CATEGORY_CONCEPT_NEW
is the name of the "BtS Concepts" category. So that can be changed to "Mods information" in CIV4GameText_advc_pedia.xml or can be moved to a different game text file and changed there. (The original definition is in CIV4GameText_Objects_BTS.xml.) The "Game Concepts" text key is TXT_KEY_PEDIA_CATEGORY_CONCEPT in CIV4GameTextInfos_Objects.xml (base game); can be re-defined to "BtS Concepts". Regarding the placement of concepts in categories, Sevopedia has these functions:
Spoiler : 
Python:
	def getNewConceptInfo(self, id):
		info = gc.getNewConceptInfo(id)
		if not self.isShortcutInfo(info) and not self.isTraitInfo(info):
			return info
		return None

	def isTraitInfo(self, info):
		return info.getType().find("_TRAIT_") != -1

	def isShortcutInfo(self, info):
		return info.getType().find("SHORTCUTS") != -1
So, if it has "_TRAIT_" or "SHORTCUTS" in its type string, it's not a BtS Concept. Everything else in BasicInfos\Civ4NewConceptInfos.xml is a BtS Concept. The pre-BtS Concepts are taken from CIV4BasicInfos.xml (the BtS version of that file). Those ConceptInfo or NewConceptInfo elements specify one text key each for the heading and for the exposition:
XML:
<ConceptInfo>
	<Type>CONCEPT_ANIMALS</Type>
	<Description>TXT_KEY_CONCEPT_ANIMALS</Description>
	<Civilopedia>TXT_KEY_CONCEPT_ANIMALS_PEDIA</Civilopedia>
</ConceptInfo>
So, to move the BtS Concepts into Game Concepts, one could copy Civ4BasicInfos.xml from BtS into the mod, cut-and-paste all the non-trait, non-shortcut NewConceptInfo elements from Civ4NewConceptInfos.xml into CIV4BasicInfos.xml and change them from NewConceptInfo to ConceptInfo. And then create NewConceptInfo elements in Civ4NewConceptInfos.xml for the content you want in there, e.g.
XML:
		<NewConceptInfo>
			<Type>CONCEPT_KMOD_RULES</Type>
			<Description>TXT_KEY_KMOD_RULES</Description>
			<Civilopedia>TXT_KEY_KMOD_RULES_PEDIA</Civilopedia>
		</NewConceptInfo>
(Could be named anything, I think; just so long as it doesn't say "trait" or "shortcut".) Plus the corresponding text key definitions in a game text file. All just in theory. Might even work if Sevopedia is disabled via the BUG menu. Ignoring that possibility, there are probably more surgical ways to achieve what you want through changes in SevoPediaMain.py. But you'd need to change a bunch of (small) functions there, not just the three I included above.
"
End of quote.

Additionally from @f1rpo's quote, i also did not need to rename
TXT KEY types nor any data so that it would include "_TRAIT_"
or "SHORTCUTS" in it.
I don't know if i did all steps correctly, but at least it works
now.
-->

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #103 -----

	<!-- Unit: extra "gold per 10% strength gain" per 1 EXP.

	     Examples (base = 25 gold per 10% strength gain, per XP = 1 gold per 10% strength gain per 1 EXP):

		 Example 1:
	     - unit has 13 EXP
	       extra gold allowance from XP = 13 XP * 1 gold per 10% strength gain per 1 EXP
	                                     = 13 gold per 10% strength gain
	       effective value = 25 gold per 10% strength gain + 13 gold per 10% strength gain
	                       = 38 gold per 10% strength gain (then capped below)

	     Example 2:
	     - unit has 20 EXP
	     - extra gold allowance from XP = 20 XP * 1 gold per 10% strength gain per 1 EXP
	                                     = 20 gold per 10% strength gain
	     - effective value = 25 gold per 10% strength gain + 20 gold per 10% strength gain
	                       = 45 gold per 10% strength gain (then capped below) -->

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #104 -----

	<!-- How to read this value: "gold per +1 flat strength gain".
	     - "flat strength gain" means raw strength points, e.g. 10 -> 13 is +3 flat strength gain.
	     - If a unit gains +1 flat strength from an upgrade, this value is the max gold the AI is willing to pay.
	     - If a unit gains +3 flat strength, the max gold is about triple, etc.

	     Important: this gate is also influenced by the XP allowance below (even at low EXP).
	     Example (base = 15 gold per +1 flat strength gain, per XP = 1 gold per +1 flat strength gain per 1 EXP):
	     - unit has 1 XP -> effective value is 15 + 1 = 16 gold per +1 flat strength gain
	     - unit has 13 XP -> effective value is 15 + 13 = 28 gold per +1 flat strength gain

	     Example (value = 15 gold per +1 flat strength gain):
	     - 10 -> 13 is +3 flat strength gain (example: Knight -> Cuirassier, strength numbers are only an example).
	       If unit has 1 EXP:
	       max gold allowed = 16 gold per +1 flat strength gain * 3 flat strength gain = 48 gold.
	       If the upgrade costs 60 gold: NO (60 > 48) -> skip.
	       If unit has 13 EXP:
	       max gold allowed = 28 gold per +1 flat strength gain * 3 flat strength gain = 84 gold.
	       If the upgrade costs 60 gold: YES (60 <= 84) -> upgrade. -->

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #105 -----

	<!--     How to read this value: "gold per 10% strength gain".
	     - If a unit gains +10% strength from an upgrade, this value is the max gold the AI is willing to pay.
	     - If a unit gains +20% strength, the max gold is about double, etc.

	     Example (value = 25 gold per 10% strength gain):
	     - 50 -> 52 is about +4% strength gain.
	       Max gold allowed for this strength gain is about:
	       25 gold per 10% strength gain * (4% strength gain / 10%) = 10 gold.
	       If the upgrade costs 20 gold: NO (20 > 10) -> skip.
	     - 3 -> 5 is about +66% strength gain.
	       Max gold allowed for this strength gain is about:
	       25 gold per 10% strength gain * (66% strength gain / 10%) = 165 gold.
	       If the upgrade costs 20 gold: YES (20 <= 165) -> upgrade. -->

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #108 -----

	<!--
	Civic switch hysteresis (anti flip-flop)

	What it does:
	- Stops the AI from bouncing between near-equal civics. The AI only switches if the
	new civic is clearly better than the current one by a small "extra lead" called slack.

	How slack is calculated:
	- slack = max( SAS_AI_DO_CIVICS_SWITCH_MIN_SLACK ,
				round( abs(current_value) * SAS_AI_DO_CIVICS_SWITCH_SLACK_PERCENT / 100 ) )

	Examples:
	- Small numbers (early game): current_value=30, MIN=5, PCT=5%
	-> slack=max(5, round(1.5))=5. New civic must be at least 30+5=35 to switch.
	- Large numbers (late game): current_value=220, MIN=5, PCT=5%
	-> slack=max(5, round(11))=11. New civic must be at least 220+11=231 to switch.

	Tuning tips:
	- Raise MIN to reduce early-game churn (e.g. 6-8). Lower it for more agility.
	- Raise PCT to require a bigger lead in late game (e.g. 6-8%). Lower it for more frequent switches.
	- Set both to 0 to disable the anti flip-flop behavior.
	-->
	<!-- Absolute floor in value-points; prevents tiny swings from causing a switch when current_value is small. Raise to reduce early-game oscillation; lower to make AI more eager. -->

		- When we’re getting such a tech, we treat it as more precious, and we’ll be willing to structure a deal where we “pay” more total value on our side.
		- When someone else is getting such a tech from us, they’ll see it as more precious as well (since their AI_techTradeVal gets the same logic when they are in danger).
	This is symmetric and “honest”: no hidden cheat, just a consistent “this is life or death, this tech is worth a lot to us” multiplier. -->

===== \Assets/XML/GameInfo/CIV4CivicInfos.xml =====
----- Comment #120 -----

			<!-- Short version: yes, if what you want is the rise of monetized, contract-based work rather than “the modern proletariat.”
				- Wage labor existed well before the Industrial Revolution (soldiers, artisans, day/harvest laborers, urban craftsmen, ship crews). It accelerates from the 13th–15th c. with coin monetization, towns, and guild production, then expands massively later.
				- Tying it to Banking (Italian city-states, credit networks, bills of exchange) is thematically solid. -->
			<!-- custom: update: slot now used by another civic, thematically fine to move this civic sooner as replied to when asking chatgpt 5.1 thanks, check if accurate -->
			<!-- Why I’d move Wage Labor to Broader Education
			Conceptually:
				- Wage labor = monetized, contract-based work, especially in towns, workshops, and proto-industry.
				- That really takes off with:
					- growing urban craft sectors and guild training
					- more widespread literacy / numeracy / bookkeeping
					- a thicker layer of “skilled workers for hire” rather than purely bonded peasants.
			In your tree, that “wider literacy / trained specialists / guild knowledge” moment is exactly what Broader Education is standing in for, while:
				- Banking = financial institutions, credit, capital flows (which you now use for Protectionism – tariff policy, external trade design).
				- Printing Press = information / communication, which already has Trade Bloc and feels like “shared treaties, leagues, common rules”. -->

===== \Assets/XML/GameInfo/CIV4GameSpeedInfo.xml =====
----- Comment #138 -----

<!-- custom: reordered in reverse from fastest at top to slowest at bottom so they appear as such in the Simple Game Game Speed section. -->
<!-- custom: timeline readability rule
	For early/midgame, prefer iMonthIncrement values that are whole years (divisible by 12),
	ideally round 5/10-year steps (i.e., multiples of 60/120 months). This avoids awkward chart
	entries like "62y6m" (example: 750 months = 62y6m). Prefer round alternatives like:
	- 720 months = 60y
	- 780 months = 65y
	and adjust iTurnsPerIncrement to keep the same segment endpoints and total turns.
	Sub-year steps (<12 months) are kept as separate rows (e.g., 6/3/2 months) for clarity. (ChatGPT 5.2 Thinking) -->
<!-- custom: note: iExtraFreeOutsideUnits is an OPTIONAL tag (schema minOccurs=0), but the DLL default was 100 (percent-style fallback).
     That default is wrong for this field because it is a UNIT COUNT added to INITIAL_FREE_OUTSIDE_UNITS, not a percent.
     Result: speeds missing the tag display/behave like "100" (see SevoPedia Game Speed Chart), while slower speeds explicitly set "1".
     We now set this tag explicitly for ALL game speeds (usually 0) to avoid hidden fallback values and keep behavior consistent.
     0 means "no extra free outside units beyond INITIAL_FREE_OUTSIDE_UNITS" and preserves the intended baseline on normal speeds (ChatGPT 5.2 Thinking). See known issue as of now 92 for details. -->
<!-- custom: also added iUnitCostPercent that was missing in some game speeds too. See known issue as of now 92 for details. -->

<!-- custom: rename "Ivory" to "Elephants". Here the main reasons for this change: 
	- 1) you need elephants to build war elephants, not ivory, even if you were to somehow find ivory somewhere, it would/could not magically turn into a war elephant, so using the animal instead.
	- 2) ivory doesn't grow on the groud i think, but is derived from elephants, independent of whether they are alive or dead.
	- 3) One could also maybe imagine that elephants the animal itself makes people happy, and not necessarily their ivory.
	Note: since they are part of the animals that are countable in english rather than uncountable (see code comment below about cattle) and seeing that civ4 doesn't seem to bother with singulars too much about animals at least. I also prefer this version. -->

===== \Assets\XML\Buildings\CIV4BuildingInfos.xml =====
----- Comment #157 -----

comment block with all or many attributes/fields infos is taken from Doto mod in C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Sid Meier's Civilization IV Beyond the Sword\Beyond the Sword\Mods\Doto\Assets\modules_old\ExtraBonus\ExtraBonus_CIV4BuildingInfos.xml, i don't know if all applies to us but good to have this info so adding it to our file, and thanks too for it, serves as additional info to confirm our hypothesis/observation/external civ4 doc/wiki read website that areacleanpower (all cities power) is indeed clean not 0 unhealthy

BuildingInfos	 This begins and ends the file. Everything must go in between these two, or it will not work.
BuildingInfo	 Encloses the entry for each building.
Advisor	Defines the type of advisor that would recommend this building in the in-game menus.
ArtDefineTag	Specifies the tag in the Civ4ArtDefines_Building file that provides graphic data for this building.
Bonus	Bonus type that speeds production.
BuildingClass	The class of the building, as defined in the Civ4BuildingClassInfos file.
CivicOption	Free civics allowed with building (for example, the Pyramids enable all Government civics).
Civilopedia	Refers to a tag in the Civ4GameText_Civilopedia_BuildingsProjects file which stores the Civilopedia description for the Building.
ConstructSound	Sound used when building is completed. See the AudioDefines, Audio2DScripts, and Audio3DScripts files.
Description	Refers to a tag in the Civ4GameTextInfos_Objects file that stores the in-game name of the Building.
FreeBonus	Provides the city with iFreeBonus (see below) number of a certain resource, like Hit Musicals (BONUS_DRAMA), Hit Songs (BONUS_MUSIC), or Hit Movies (BONUS_MOVIES).
FreeBuilding	Provides the listed building free to every city. For example, the Three Gorges Dam gives a Hydro Station to each city.
FreePromotion	Allows building to give listed promotion to all units built in that city, like Red Cross and Medic I.
FreeStartEra	Building is free if you start in this Era or later.
GlobalReligionCommerce	Earns gold from every city with this religion.
GreatPeopleUnitClass	Type of Great Person Points the building generates.
HolyCity	If set, the building can only be constructed in the Holy City of the religion specified.
MaxStartEra	Latest Era the building can be constructed.
MovieDefineTag	Specifies the tag in the Civ4ArtDefines_Movie file that will play when this building is constructed.
NoBonus	The building blocks the resource from being accessed in the city. (Ex: National Park blocks coal).
ObsoleteTech	Tech that makes the building obsolete (like Economics for Castles).
PowerBonus	Any building with this bonus supplies power to the given city (i.e. BONUS_COAL for the Coal Plant).
PrereqBonuses	Bonus type required for production.
PrereqReligion	Defines Religion needed for production.
ReligionType	If set, the Building can only be constructed if the specified Religion type is present in a city within the Civilization (though not necessarily in the city where it is being constructed).
SpecialBuildingType	Determines whether or not the building is one the special types listed in the Civ4SpecialBuildingInfos file, like a monastery or cathedral.
StateReligion	If set, the Building can only be constructed while the specified Religion type is the Civilization's State Religion.
Strategy	Refers to a tag in the Civ4GameText_Strategy file that stores the strategic or usage info on the Building, displayed in mouseover pop-ups.
Type	The type of Building (see the Civ4ArtDefines_Building file).
VictoryPrereq	Victory type required for construction of the building (i.e. the Diplomacy victory condition must be enabled for the United Nations to be available).
DiploVoteType	If one is set, the building will allow diplomatic voting of the votes allowed by that vote type; used by the United Nations and the Apostolic Palace.
iAirlift	The distance from city that units can be airlifted.
iAirModifier	Damage change from air units.
iAIWeight	Importance of building to the AI.
iAllCityDefense	% change in defense in all cities (e.g. Chichen Itza increases defenses by 25% ).
iAnarchyModifier	% change in anarchy length.
iAreaHappiness	Change in happiness in all cities on this continent.
iAreaHealth	Change in healthiness in all cities on this continent.
iAreaFreeSpecialist	Free specialists allowed in all cities on this continent (e.g. Statue of Liberty grants +1 specialist per city).
iAsset	Point value of building for victory score.
iBombardDefense	Percent reduction in damage to defense a city can get from bombardment from non-gunpowder units
iCitiesPrereq	Requisite number of cities for this building.
iCoastalTradeRoutes	Increases the number of trade routes in all coastal cities by this amount.
iConquestProb	Percentage chance building will survive if city is captured.
iCost	Building cost in hammers.
iDefense	% change in this city's defense bonus.
iExperience	Experience bonus to units built in this city.
iFoodKept	% of food kept after city grows.
iFreeSpecialist	Free specialists allowed in this city.
iFreeTechs	Number of free techs provided when built.
iGlobalExperience	Experience bonus to units built in all cities (e.g. the Pentagon gives +2 XP to all units).
iGlobalFreeSpecialist	Free specialists allowed in all cities.
iGlobalGreatPeopleRateModifier	% increase of Great Person Points generation rate for civilization.
iGlobalHappiness	Change in happiness in all cities.
iGlobalHealth	Change in healthiness in all cities.
iGlobalHurryModifier	% change in hurry cost in all cities.
iGlobalPopulationChange	Change in population globally.
iGlobalSpaceProductionModifier	% change in spaceship parts production in all cities.
iGlobalTradeRoutes	Change in number of trade routes in all cities.
iGlobalWarWearinessModifier	% change in war weariness in all cities.
iGreatPeopleRateChange	Integer change in Great Person Points generation (i.e. used for wonders, like the Pyramid's +2 Great Engineer points per turn).
iGreatPeopleRateModifier	% increase of Great Person Points generation rate in this city.
iHappiness	Change in happiness in this city.
iHealRateChange	% change in healing rates.
iHealth	Change in healthiness in this city.
iHurryCostModifier	Building hurry cost modifier (e.g. a value of 100 makes the building, usually a wonder, twice as expensive to rush via gold or population).
iLevelPrereq	Requisite unit level (e.g. a level 6 unit is required for West Point).
iMaintenanceModifier	% change in maintenance cost.
iMaxLatitude	Maximum distance from equator.
iMilitaryProductionModifier	% change in military units' production.
iMinAreaSize	Size of adjacent body of water for sea buildings (usually 10).
iMinLatitude	Minimum distance from equator.
iNukeModifier	Damage change from nukes.
iNukeExplosionRand	Chance that a nuclear power plant will randomly blow up, causing a meltdown (default 1 in 1000).
iNumFreeBonuses	Connected to the FreeBonus tag, this determines how many of the specified resource to provide. -1 gives 5 of the above bonuses. Any number >= 0 is itself, i.e. 10 here with a BONUS_DRAMA in the FreeBonus tag means 10 Hit Musicals from your city. Anything below -1 shows up as that negative number.
iPower	Value used by AI to calculate a player's relative strength (Forge, Heroic Epic, etc. influence this).
iSpaceProductionModifier	% change in spaceship parts' production.
iStateReligionHappiness	Change in happiness with state religion.
iTeamsPrereq	Instances among a team in multiplayer (e.g. the Apostolic Palace and United Nations have the value 3, while most buildings have a value of 0).
iTradeRouteModifier	% change in trade routes' yield in this city.
iTradeRoutes	Change in number of trade routes in this city.
iWarWearinessModifier	% change in war weariness.
iWorkerSpeedModifier	% change in worker speed.
bAllowsNukes	Allows nukes for all players.
bAreaCleanPower	Provides clean power to all cities on same continent (e.g. Three Gorges Dam).
bBorderObstacle	If Barbarians can no longer cross the players borders.
bBuildingOnlyHealthy	Removes unhealthiness caused by buildings (e.g. used by Recycling Center).
bCapital	Sets the Capital City. There can only be one per civilization.
bCenterInCity	Appears to locate the building at the center of the city. Buildings with a value of 1 for this tag include Aqueduct, Castle, and Monument.
bDiploVote	Allows diplomatic votes (e.g. the United Nations); Vanilla and Warlords version of DiploVoteType
bDirtyPower	Power provided is polluting.
bForceTeamVoteEligible	Forces team members to vote with you.
bGoldenAge	Starts a golden age (e.g. used by Taj Mahal).
bGovernmentCenter	Makes the city a center of government (multiple allowed per civilization). Used by the Versailles wonder.
bMapCentering	On completion, centers the map.
bNeverCapture	Indicates if the building is destroyed when the city is captured.
bNoUnhappiness	Removes all unhappiness from the city. Used by the Globe Theatre.
bNoUnhealthyPopulation	Removes all unhealthiness from population. Used by the National Park.
bNukeImmune	Makes the building immune to nuking.
bPower	Provides power.
bPrereqReligion	Indicates if a Religion must be present.
bRiver	Requires river.
bTeamShare	Allows team members to build together.
bWater	Requires water.
BonusHappinessChanges	This defines the changes in happiness provided by the building if you have the specified resources. The tag BonusHappinessChange, bracketed by BonusHappinessChanges, contains each individual resource and its effect. BonusType defines the type of resource, and iHappinessChange defines the amount of happiness increase.
BonusHealthChanges	This defines the changes in healthiness provided by the building if you have the specified resources. The tag BonusHealthChange, bracketed by BonusHealthChanges, contains each individual resource and its effect. BonusType defines the type of resource, and iHealthChange defines the amount of health increase.
BonusProductionModifiers	This controls the production bonuses for the building as a result of specified resources. BonusProductionModifier contains each individual resources and its effect, BonusType defines the type of resource, and iProductionModifier is a percentage value defining the change in production (e.g. Walls have a value for BONUS_TIMBER of 100).
BonusYieldModifiers	This defines the bonuses to resources' yields from a building. BonusYieldModifier declares each resource, BonusType defines the type of resource, YieldModifiers brackets the three yield tags, and iYield controls the percentage change in food, hammer, and commerce production in that city.
BuildingClassNeededs	This tag defines the buildings in the city that are required for the particular city improvement. BuildingClassNeeded defines the individual building classes, BuildingClassType declares the specific type of building, bNeededInCity is a Boolean tag controlling whether the building is needed in this city, and BuildingInfo brackets the entire building group.
BuildingHappinessChanges	Defines which other buildings get happiness when this one is built.
CommerceChangeDoubleTimes	Defines how many turns must pass before a given commerce output is doubled. For example, Versailles starts giving 10 Culture per turn, but after 1000 years, begins giving 20 Culture per turn. The three iCommerce tags refer to beakers, gold, and culture per turn.
CommerceChangeOriginalOwners	Defines the increases in beakers, gold, culture, and espionage per turn that the building provides, using the iCommerce integer tag. Only the player that builds it gets the specified commerce changes.
CommerceChanges	Defines the increases in beakers, gold, culture, and espionage per turn that the building provides, using the iCommerce integer tag.
CommerceFlexibles	Allows players to adjust the commerce rate, just like technologies.
CommerceHappinesses	This determines the percentage change in tax/research/culture sliders' rates, respectively, that produce happy faces, using the iCommerce tag. This is used for buildings like the Theater and Hippodrome.
CommerceModifiers	Percentage change to the commerce production for the entire city (e.g. 25 is 25% increase), using the iCommerce tag. The first tag controls beakers per turn, the second gold per turn, the third culture per turn, and the fourth in espionage per turn.
DomainFreeExperiences	This tag defines the experience bonus given to units of a certain type. The DomainFreeExperience tag contains each unit type, DomainType declares whether the unit is a land, sea, or air unit, and iExperience is an integer tag defining how many XP points are given to that unit type.
DomainProductionModifiers	This tag defines the production bonus given to units of a certain type. The DomainFreeExperience tag contains each unit type, DomainType declares whether the unit is a land, sea, or air unit, and iProductionModifier defines the percentage bonus given to unit construction for that type.
Flavors	Used by the AI to decide what buildings to build based on personality
FreeSpecialistCounts	This tag lists the free specialists given to a city by the particular building. The FreeSpecialistCount brackets the info for each specialist type (Spy, Engineer, Merchant, etc.), SpecialistType declares the individual specialist type, and iFreeSpecialistCount controls how many free specialists of that type are granted.
GlobalSeaPlotYieldChanges	Change in sea square yield for all cities, using the iYield integer tag.
HotKey	Essentially useless. This relies on the Boolean tags bAltDown, bCtrlDown, and bShiftDown.
ObsoleteSafeCommerceChanges	Additions to commerce outputs that aren't affected by other changes and the building going obsolete. Used primarily for culture per turn, this relies on the iCommerce integer tag.
PrereqBuildingClasses	This specifies the amount of certain building class needed to construct this city improvement. PrereqBuildingClass brackets the info for each building class, BuildingClassType declares the building type, and iNumBuildingNeeded states the number of that building type required.
ProductionTraits	This tag contains all the leader traits' production bonuses for the building. For example, Aggressive leaders receive a bonus when building a Barracks. ProductionTrait contains the info for each trait, ProductionTraitType defines the particular trait type, and iProductionTrait declares the percentage bonus to production.
ReligionChanges	Similar to HOLY_CITY_INFLUENCE in GlobalDefines.xml, sets the change in religious influence the city has, which is used when determining religion spread. ReligionChange contains the info for each religion, ReligionType indicates the particular religion affecting the building, and iReligionChange is an integer tag used to affect this building.
SeaPlotYieldChanges	This defines the change in sea squares' yield, using the iYield integer tag for food, hammers, and commerce output.
RiverPlotYieldChanges	This defines the change in river squares' yield, using the iYield integer tag for food, hammers, and commerce output.
SpecialistCounts	This list tag controls how many of each type of specialist this building allows (e.g. a Library allows only 2 Scientists). SpecialistCount contains the info for each specialist, SpecialistType declares the specialist type, and iSpecialistCount defines how many of that type are permitted.
SpecialistExtraCommerces	Bonus provided to specialists by the building, uses iCommerce tag (e.g. Sistine Chapel provides extra culture per turn to each specialist).
SpecialistYieldChanges	This controls the bonuses given to each specialist type by the building in question. SpecialistYieldChange contains all of the tags for each particular specialist: SpecialistType declares the type of specialist (Spy, Engineer, Merchant, etc.) and YieldChanges uses the iYield integer tag to modify Food, Hammers, and Gold per turn output.
StateReligionCommerces	Bonus provided by buildings of the state religion, uses iCommerce tag (again used by the Sistine Chapel to increase culture per turn output of religous buildings).
TechTypes	This tag lists all technologies that are required to allow construction. Note that you must have all of these techs beforehand (i.e. these are AND techs, not OR techs). The PrereqTech tag is used to bracket each individual prerequisite.
UnitCombatFreeExperiences	This controls experience point bonuses to units of a certain combat type (Siege, Mounted, Melee, etc.). UnitCombatFreeExperience groups the information for each unit type, and the iExperience integer tag describes how much free XP to give to that type.
YieldChanges	Defines the change in city yield, using iYield. There are three iYield tags: the first is for food per turn change, the second for hammers per turn, and the third for commerce per turn (e.g. the Palace provides 8 free commerce per turn).
YieldModifiers	Percentage change to the yields for the entire city (e.g. 25 is 25% increase), using the iYield tag. The first tag controls food per turn, the second hammers per turn, and the third commerce per turn. (e.g the Forge provides 25% more hammers per turn)
fVisibilityPriority	Used to give certain buildings priority when drawing them on the map. Most buildings have a value of 1.0, but the Aqueduct, for example, as a value of 10240000.0 in order to ensure that it is always drawn first.

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #166 -----

For example if we have computed that max allowed for our empire without excess is 1 trbuchet, and if the below is set to 2, then the min for our empire will be 1 + 2 = 3 trebuchet-like units and we won't block excess production of trebuchets-like until >= 3 for example. As i find them very inefficient for AIs due to being useless outside of raiding, and AIs often overbuilding them, i set very low or low enough tresholds specifically more for trebuchets-like units, adjust as you see fit, and setting this very high is about the same as setting the no excess trebuchets toggle (except for checks like if enemy strong and at war, if in danger, then avoid them as they are less efficient or most likely to be useful and versatile in most cases (bad at defense especially for AI))

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #176 -----

	     Unit: extra "gold per +1 flat strength gain" per 1 EXP.

	     Example (per XP value = 1 gold per +1 flat strength gain per 1 EXP):
	     - unit has 1 EXP
	       extra gold allowance from XP = 1 XP * 1 gold per +1 flat strength gain per 1 EXP
	                                     = 1 gold per +1 flat strength gain
	     - unit has 13 EXP
	       extra gold allowance from XP = 13 XP * 1 gold per +1 flat strength gain per 1 EXP
	                                     = 13 gold per +1 flat strength gain

	     Effective value used by the flat strength gain gate:
	     - effective value = base gold per +1 flat strength gain + extra gold allowance from XP

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #177 -----

	     Reason: if upgrades get too expensive, it is often better to build new units instead (early units are cheap to rebuild, late-game tiny gains are not worth big gold).
	     Unit: max "gold per 10% strength gain" after adding the XP allowance.

	     Example (cap = 50 gold per 10% strength gain):
	     - base + XP would give 120 gold per 10% strength gain
	     - cap is 50 gold per 10% strength gain
	     - final effective value becomes 50 gold per 10% strength gain -->

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #179 -----

, moluscs have +1 health and +1 with harbor but not all cities are coastal so +1.5 health effectively in terms of how much we value it more or less for example (see code for updated values), or grapes giving +1 happy and +1 happy with grocer so effectively +2 happy) as soon as the buyer has the tech required to build these buildings (we assume that then all his cities have the building to simplify and quite accurate still i think as such). AI prices vary with various factors outside of this code, but generall it could lead for example to wheat being valued 4-5 gold per turn vs cow 2 gold per turn, unlike before where both would be value 2 gold per turn which was nonsensical and easy to abuse. ----- Comment #181 -----

----- Comment #186 -----

enable so that even UNITAI_COUNTER default unitai units (e.g. as of now the greek hoplite) are equally inflated (i.e. valorized) in the fallback unit code, so the AI players values them if buildable (even though they are generally less effective at offense (but they as civ-specific units should have in one way or another "good"/stronger stats than base units (else rebalance them) so it is maybe fine too to valorize them as well, with here the option to disable it if you prefer)). In other words, as of now between for example a greek_hoplite and and a generic unit_longbowman, enable this to choose the greek_hoplite (as it is more efficient in hammer cost, and versatile enough) rather than the longbowman as a fallback unit, but between a spearman and a longbowman as fallback unit, still favour the longbowman (as it is more versatile and slightly stronger as well even if more expensive better than stockpiling weaker units and go bankrupt)

===== \Assets/XML/GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml =====
----- Comment #191 -----

<!-- custom: for our new unlimited specialists valuation code, it is great at spreading more evenly civics at various stages of the game vs only civic_caste_system before, but we switch a bit too often. Yet, it is not as attractive to change civics if we'd have anarchy, so add some devaluation depending on anarchy length. E.g. if civic value is 1000 (random number as an example) and this knob's value is 10(%), and we have 2 anarchy turns to change civic, then the new civic value would be ((100 - (2 * 10)) * 1000) / 100 = (80 * 1000) / 100 = 800. But if anarchy length is 0 turns, then value remains 1000 and as attractive, as ((100 - (0 * 10)) * 1000) / 100 = 100 * 1000 / 100 = 1000 (100% value is same). In short, for example -10% civic value loss per turn of anarchy. -->

===== \Assets/XML/Text/CIV4GameText_advc.xml =====
----- Comment #266 -----

	<!-- advc.005b: From CIV4GameText_Warlords.xml.
		 I'm removing the roman numerals. It's clear enough e.g. which
		 Gustav Adolph is meant, and if not, I don't mind the ambuigity.
		 They're not in the game as rulers (those are the AI leaders),
		 but as generals. Also for that reason, Boudica and Charlemagne are
		 replaced. Wanted famous replacements in about the same period,
		 and of the same gender. The Vietnamese Hai Ba Trung is to
		 conceal the fact that they're two people - the two "Ladies
		 Trung". Players who know about the Trung rebellion won't mind,
		 and those who don't won't be confused by their GG supposedly
		 consisting of two persons. Attila is already included through
		 the Huns event - would've been an obvious replacement for
		 Charlemagne otherwise. Went with Jan Zizka - don't know if
		 he's famous enough. Other ideas from High/Late Medieval times:
		 Eulji Mundeok, Tancred, Richard Lionheart. -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #280 -----

		<!-- All other elements are optional (minOccurs 0). So that mods with
			 additional civs need to define only the geo coordinates,
			 which are easy to look up on Wikipedia (though the minutes stated
			 there will have to be converted to tenths of degrees through
			 multiplication by 5/3). -->
		<!-- Annual, in millimeters. I've mostly tried to find the present-day
			 precipitation average of the modern country or countries
			 corresponding to the civ.
			 This translates into a preference for terrain and feature types.
			 The ids of those types are obtained from the Global Warming
			 definitions in GlobalDefinesAlt.xml. -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #281 -----

		<!-- A percentage expressing how much the climate varies across the
			 (core) territory of the civilization. Again, this results in a
			 bias for terrain and feature types, so not all kinds of variations
			 should be equally reflected by the percentage. Large civs should
			 tend to have higher variation values (because players will
			 associate them with diverse biomes), but the degree of variation
			 within smaller subareas should also matter. For example, Western
			 Russia does not have much variation, whereas Korea is quite varied
			 for a small country. -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #282 -----

		<!-- Percentage expressing how maritime the civ is. The game -mostly-
			 aims at that ratio of sea tiles (or land tiles on a different
			 landmass than the starting tiles) among the tiles surrounding the
			 starting tile. To choose the values, I've looked at the
			 approximate ratios on Earth within a 550 km radius around the
			 capitals (that surface area, conveniently, covers about 1 mio.
			 square kilometers), then made adjustments based on the capitals'
			 distance from the coast, length of the coastline and the civs'
			 naval histories. -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #283 -----

		<!-- Percentage expressing the importance of having a major river
			 (as in: one of the world's biggest or most prominent river systems)
			 near the civ's starting tile. 0 means that it doesn't matter
			 whether there's a major river. A negative percentage discourages
			 starts near major rivers. The default value is -25. (Civs not
			 strongly associated with a river should, ideally, have only
			 minor rivers near their starting plots.) -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #284 -----

		<!-- Highest elevation in meters that is within the historical
			 territory of the civ and not too far away from its core.
			 Translates into an affinity for Peaks.
			 Using prominence, or the highest elevation minus the average of
			 the civ's whole (core) territory, would be slightly more helpful,
			 but it really doesn't make much of a difference for the civs that
			 we have, and maximal elevation is easy to research. (I've mainly
			 used a table of extreme elevations per country on Wikipedia.) -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #285 -----

		<!-- Percentage of the civ's core area that has a mountainous relief.
			 (This is a matter of gradients, not absolute elevation. E.g. an
			 altiplano is not considered mountainous in this context.)
			 Translates into an affinity for hills and peaks.
			 (I use crude approximations based on some world map showing that
			 info on a per-country basis.) -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #286 -----

		<!-- In units of 1000 square kilometer. Hard to say which borders to
			 use. Results in a (slight) bias for or against starting tiles
			 with (somewhat) more space for expansion than the average.
			 The specific numbers don't matter much, just the order of
			 magnitude. For empires, the largest extent can vastly exceed
			 the pre-imperial or core area. I've been picking modest sizes
			 in these cases, also taking into account whether the conquered
			 peoples are represented in the game as civs. In any case, a civ
			 should have to do some actual conquering to become a veritable
			 empire. However, small sizes, say, less than 250 thousand square
			 kilometers, should be reserved for civs that are only thought of
			 as small, including colonial empires like Portugal. -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #287 -----

		<!-- Target ratio of the horizontal to the vertical dimension of the
			 civ's space for expansion, as a percentage. E.g. 150 means that
			 the civ should have 50% more space for expansion in west-east
			 direction than in north-south direction. Should not necessarily
			 be used for civs that expanded into a particular direction as
			 empires (e.g. Persia) because having space in that direction
			 can mean that they'll conquer neighboring civs in just the
			 perpendicular (counter-historical) direction. I'm also never
			 setting a preference for civs with a small area because their
			 shape didn't matter much historically. For large civs, like
			 Egypt, the shape mattered more as it caused them to span multiple
			 climate zones. -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #288 -----

		<!-- To make preferences for bonus resources easier to specify.
			 Use ARTSTYLE_... strings here to avoid having to define a new
			 global type for the regions. Using the artstyles already assigned
			 for CivilizationInfos wouldn't (always) work since those are based
			 on culture, not geography. Moreover, a mod might add and assign
			 new artstyles (but I doubt that it would eliminate any of the
			 existing ones). -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #289 -----

		<!-- Gregorian year number. Negative sign for BC(E). When in doubt,
			 it should be the year in which the leader took control of the bulk
			 of his or her domain. Should ideally also have ruled for several
			 years after. Optional - the DLL treats the date as (entirely) unknown
			 when none is stated. -->

===== \Assets/XML/TrueStarts/CIV4TrueStartsSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #290 -----

	<!-- Always nice to have some means of encouraging or discouraging a
		 a particular outcome for no particular reason. +100 is a "high"
		 bias in favor (but not the highest possible), -100 is a
		 maximal bias against. -->

			- Monotonic: years/turn never go up as time advances (they step down to 1y/turn at the end).

			-->

			- Same early fade as Normal/Marathon. First 7 bands reuse Normal’s 5,000→2,500→1,000→…→125 y/turn steps, so Prehistory isn’t over-detailed.
			- Clean math. Every ≥1y step is an integer; months/turn are all multiples of 12; only the two final bands use 6-month turns.
			- Monotonic. Years/turn never increase as time advances.
			- Pivots line up. 1500/1750/1790/1880/1900/1980/1990/2040/2070/2080/2105 stay intact.
			- Exactly 750 turns. (I checked the sum.)

===== \Assets\XML\GameInfo\CIV4GameInfoSchema.xml =====
----- Comment #354 -----

		<!-- advc: New game speed modifiers ...
			 Set to 100 in the DLL by default. -->
		<!-- advc.130r: start -->
		<!-- Affects the per-turn chance of incrementing or decrementing AI diplo
			 memory counters. (Some count up, e.g. "years of peace", others decay,
			 namely remembered diplo actions such as "you declared war on us".)
			 Percentages above 100 make it less(!) likely for counters to be
			 changed, i.e. make diplomacy more inert.
			 During the early game, counters that count up receive
			 special treatment: they get adjusted to game length and the
			 MemoryRand adjustment only takes effect gradually. -->

			 Peace offers and requests for joining a war are exempt from

===== /Assets/XML/GameInfo/CIV4GameSpeedInfo.xml =====
----- Comment #358 -----

<!-- custom: timeline rebalance guidelines (shared by Marathon/Slow/Very Slow). (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 0) Normal is the style baseline: big round year-steps first (5k/2.5k/1k/750/500/250/125 ...), then smaller whole-year bands, and only a late month-phase (m6) near the end. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 0b) This creates an immersive, natural-feeling calendar instead of noisy precision early on. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 1) Before entering the sub-year phase (<= 12 months/turn), increments should stay divisible by 12 so checkpoint dates remain on whole years; e.g. 60 is valid (5 years/turn), because 60 % 12 == 0. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 2) Prefer round, human-readable year bands first (e.g. 2000, 1000, 500, 300, 150, 75, 60, 40, 20, 10), then finer odd-year tuning only when necessary. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 3) When using 5-based year steps (e.g. 75/25/15/5), prefer even turn counts or matching follow-up bands so boundary years still land on clean trailing digits (..0/..5), avoiding ugly drift patterns like ..5 -> ..3 -> ..1 -> ..9. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 4) Avoid yo-yo sequences (e.g. 2->1->2->1) and abrupt cliffs by adding intermediate steps when needed so the curve feels gradual to players. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 5) Target close-enough alignment to Normal, not exact matching, when exactness conflicts with cleaner/natural-looking progression. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 6) For slower speeds, choose a readable increment ladder first (e.g. 3000/2400/1800/1200/900/600/480/360/240/180/144/120/96/84/72/60...), then tune counts; avoid hard drops like ~3000 -> 360 without intermediate bands. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 7) If one increment band consumes too much of the timeline, split it into smaller adjacent bands (e.g. 360 -> 240 -> 120 instead of a long 360 block) to improve pacing control and avoid late overcorrection. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 8) When a band overshoots/overcompensates, redistribute excess to nearby bands instead of forcing distant corrections (e.g. reduce 360 turns and increase 240 turns, or vice versa), so pacing stays local and smooth. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 9) Outside Normal, exact "beautiful key dates" (e.g. precise 1400/1900 anchors) are a soft preference, not a hard rule; prioritize close pace and readable enough increments. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 10) Prefer swap-friendly neighboring increments when practical (e.g. 120<->60, 12<->6, 3<->1) because integer redistribution is easier and cleaner (1x120 = 2x60, 1x3 = 3x1), which helps absorb local overshoot without yo-yo. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 11) Keep timeline length anchored when tuning: compensate month adds with equivalent month removes (example: +1 turn at m12 must be offset by -2 turns at m6 somewhere else, and still keep total turn count 1500 via late-band redistribution such as m4<->m3). (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->
<!-- custom: 12) Avoid "ugly" standalone year bands like 220y when nearby round bands can express the same region more naturally; prefer local mixes of pretty steps (e.g. 300/250/200/180 years) over a long single 220y block, as long as total turns/months and checkpoint pacing remain controlled. (GPT-5.3-Codex) -->

===== /Assets/XML/Units/CIV4UnitInfos.xml =====
----- Comment #359 -----

set a tech to block training once any team knows it (human + AI); existing units remain, but can be scrapped if ObsoleteTech is known.
Reason: we disable broad scrapping for hammer efficiency; ObsoleteTech gives a per-unit gate instead of scattered rules.
Use it to retire early units that are cheap but upkeep-inefficient later (e.g., warrior -> TECH_BRONZE_WORKING).
If you change upgrade chains or era pacing, update ObsoleteTech to match the tech where the unit should stop being built.
If the tag is missing or set to NONE, the unit remains buildable forever (workers/settlers or timeless special units).
Why: this makes unit XML portable between mods — units without the tag keep old behavior, while mods that add it gain a clean stop-build gate.
Note: we include ObsoleteTech on every UnitInfo (default NONE) to make edits consistent and easy to search. (GPT-5.2-Codex)
